In 2019, Spielberg filmed *[West Side Story](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Side_Story_\(2021_film\))*, an adaptation of the [musical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Side_Story) by [Leonard Bernstein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leonard_Bernstein) and [Stephen Sondheim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_Sondheim).[^cite_note-254] It stars [Ansel Elgort](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ansel_Elgort) and [Rachel Zegler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rachel_Zegler) in her film debut, with [Ariana DeBose](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ariana_DeBose), [David Alvarez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Alvarez_\(actor\)), [Mike Faist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_Faist) and [Rita Moreno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rita_Moreno) in supporting roles. Written by Tony Kushner, the film stays true to the original 1950s setting.[^cite_note-255] *West Side Story* was released in December 2021 to positive reviews and received seven Academy Award nominations, including Best Picture and Best Director.[^cite_note-nytimes-oscars-256] Spielberg also received nominations from the [Golden Globe Awards](142724.md), [Directors Guild of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Directors_Guild_of_America), and [Critics' Choice Movie Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Critics'_Choice_Movie_Awards).[^cite_note-257] *[The Economist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Economist)* praised the choreography, stating that it "stunningly melds beauty and violence".[^cite_note-258] In March 2022, Spielberg said that *West Side Story* would be his last musical.[^cite_note-259]

Spielberg's *[The Fabelmans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Fabelmans)* (2022) is a self-portrait of the director as a young man, which he wrote with Kushner.[^cite_note-260] [Gabriel LaBelle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gabriel_LaBelle) plays [Sammy Fabelman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sammy_Fabelman), an aspiring filmmaker. [Michelle Williams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michelle_Williams_\(actress\)) plays his mother, Mitzi; [Paul Dano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_Dano) his father, Burt; [Seth Rogen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seth_Rogen) plays Burt's best friend Bennie Loewy and [Judd Hirsch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judd_Hirsch) plays Mitzi's Uncle Boris. It features a cameo from [David Lynch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Lynch).[^cite_note-261][^cite_note-262] Filming began in Los Angeles in July 2021, and it premiered at the [2022 Toronto International Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Toronto_International_Film_Festival) on September 10, Spielberg's debut at that festival.[^cite_note-263] It received widespread critical acclaim and won the festival's [People's Choice Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toronto_International_Film_Festival_People's_Choice_Award).[^cite_note-264] It received a limited theatrical release on November 11, 2022, by [Universal Pictures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universal_Pictures), before expanding wide on November 23.[^cite_note-265]



Spielberg at [Berlinale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berlinale) at 2023

Despite favorable critical reception, *West Side Story* and *The Fabelmans* were box-office failures, which *[Variety](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Variety_\(magazine\))* suggested could be attributed to a decline in the popularity of Spielberg in a film-going environment altered by the [COVID-19 pandemic](62750956.md), and the public's loss of interest in [prestige films](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prestige_picture).[^cite_note-266] *The Fabelmans* received seven Academy Award nominations, including Best Picture, Best Director and Best Original Screenplay.[^cite_note-267][^cite_note-268] It was, however, a major box office success in France and became the highest-rated film of the 21st century in the country, with a 4.9 average from critics on [AlloCiné](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AlloCin%C3%A9) from 43 reviews, with all but six giving it 5 stars. *[Cahiers du Cinéma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cahiers_du_Cin%C3%A9ma)* wrote that Spielberg had "come to represent like no other, the idea of cinema as wonder, at a time when the relationship to the spectacular and the cinema seems more tormented than ever" and declared that it will "undoubtedly remain the most important and singular film of his career".[^cite_note-269][^cite_note-270]

Spielberg had planned to direct *[Indiana Jones and the Dial of Destiny](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Jones_and_the_Dial_of_Destiny)*, but withdrew and was replaced by [James Mangold](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Mangold). Spielberg said that he would remain "hands on" as a producer,[^cite_note-271][^cite_note-272] along with Kathleen Kennedy and Frank Marshall. In 2016, it was announced that it would be written by David Koepp,[^cite_note-273] with a release by Disney on July 19, 2019.[^cite_note-274] After a change of filming and release dates,[^cite_note-275][^cite_note-276] it was postponed again when [Jonathan Kasdan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jonathan_Kasdan) was announced as the film's new writer.[^cite_note-277] Soon after, a new release date of July 9, 2021, was announced.[^cite_note-278] In May 2019, [Dan Fogelman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dan_Fogelman) was hired to write a new script, and Kasdan's story, focused on the [Nazi gold train](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazi_gold_train), would not be used; the script was ultimately credited to Mangold, Koepp, [Jez Butterworth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jez_Butterworth), and [John-Henry Butterworth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John-Henry_Butterworth).[^cite_note-279] In April 2020, it was announced that the release was delayed to July 29, 2022, due to the [COVID-19 pandemic](62750956.md),[^cite_note-280] and in October 2021, the release date was again delayed to June 30, 2023.[^cite_note-281] The film began production in the [UK](31717.md) in June 2021[^cite_note-282] and finished in February 2022.[^cite_note-283]

In February 2025, Spielberg began shooting *[Disclosure Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disclosure_Day)*,[^cite_note-284] about UFOs. The screenplay was written by Koepp, from an original idea from Spielberg.[^cite_note-285] It stars [Emily Blunt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emily_Blunt), [Josh O'Connor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Josh_O'Connor), [Colman Domingo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colman_Domingo), [Colin Firth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colin_Firth), [Wyatt Russell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wyatt_Russell) and [Eve Hewson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eve_Hewson),[^cite_note-286] and was released in theatres on June 12, 2026, by [Universal Pictures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universal_Pictures).[^cite_note-287][^cite_note-288][^cite_note-289]

## Other ventures

### Production

See also: [Amblin Entertainment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amblin_Entertainment), [DreamWorks Pictures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DreamWorks_Pictures), [DreamWorks Animation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DreamWorks_Animation), and [Amblin Partners](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amblin_Partners)

Spielberg's first film as an executive producer was the directorial debut of [Robert Zemeckis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Zemeckis), [*I Wanna Hold Your Hand*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/I_Wanna_Hold_Your_Hand_\(film\)) (1978).[^cite_note-Thomson-13] He produced Zemeckis's [dark comedy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dark_comedy) *[Used Cars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Used_Cars)* (1980), which was a critical but not a commercial success. In 1980, Spielberg, [Kathleen Kennedy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kathleen_Kennedy_\(producer\)) and [Frank Marshall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frank_Marshall_\(filmmaker\)) founded [Amblin Productions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amblin_Productions); the first film it produced was the [romantic comedy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romantic_comedy) [*Continental Divide*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Continental_Divide_\(film\)) ([Michael Apted](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Apted), 1981).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJackson200741-290] It went on to produce *[Gremlins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gremlins)* ([Joe Dante](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joe_Dante), 1984), *[The Goonies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Goonies)* ([Richard Donner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Donner), 1985), *[Back to the Future](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Back_to_the_Future)* (Zemeckis, 1985), *[Who Framed Roger Rabbit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Who_Framed_Roger_Rabbit)* (Zemeckis, 1988), [*Arachnophobia*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arachnophobia_\(film\)) (Marshall, 1990), *[Joe Versus the Volcano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joe_Versus_the_Volcano)* ([John Patrick Shanley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Patrick_Shanley), 1990), *[Twister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twister_\(1996_film\))* ([Jan de Bont](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jan_de_Bont), 1996), [*Men in Black*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Men_in_Black_\(1997_film\)) ([Barry Sonnenfeld](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barry_Sonnenfeld), 1997) and *[The Mask of Zorro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Mask_of_Zorro)* ([Martin Campbell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Campbell), 1998).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcBride1997380-291][^cite_note-Corliss-292]*[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJackson2007105-293]*[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcBride1997506-294] For some, including *[Young Sherlock Holmes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Young_Sherlock_Holmes)* ([Barry Levinson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barry_Levinson), 1985) and *[Harry and the Hendersons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harry_and_the_Hendersons)* ([William Dear](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Dear), 1987), the title "Steven Spielberg Presents" was in the opening credits.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcBride1997381-295] It produced [Don Bluth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Don_Bluth)'s animated films *[An American Tail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/An_American_Tail)* (1986) and *[The Land Before Time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Land_Before_Time_\(film\))* (1988), leading to the spin-off [Amblimation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amblimation).[^cite_note-Corliss-292][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcBride1997506-294][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJackson2007105-293] In 1985, [NBC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBC) offered Spielberg a two-year contract on a television series, *[Amazing Stories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amazing_Stories_\(1985_TV_series\));* the show was marketed as a blend of *[The Twilight Zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Twilight_Zone)* and *[Alfred Hitchcock Presents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alfred_Hitchcock_Presents)*. NBC gave Spielberg creative control and a budget of \$1 million for each episode.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcBride1997389-296] After two seasons and disappointing ratings, the show was not renewed.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcBride1997390-297] Although Spielberg's involvement as a producer would vary widely from project to project, Zemeckis said that Spielberg would always "respect the filmmaker's vision".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcBride1997384-298] Over the next decade, Spielberg's record as a producer brought mixed critical and commercial results.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcBride1997384-298] He produced cartoons such as [Brad Bird](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brad_Bird)'s *[Family Dog](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Family_Dog_\(TV_series\))* and [Tom Ruegger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tom_Ruegger)'s *[Animaniacs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Animaniacs)*, *[Freakazoid!](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freakazoid!)* and *[Pinky and the Brain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pinky_and_the_Brain)*.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcBride1997507-299] In 1992, Spielberg began to scale back producing, saying "Producing has been the least fulfilling aspect of what I've done in the last decade."[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcBride1997386-300] He produced the documentary *[A Brief History of Time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A_Brief_History_of_Time_\(film\))* by [Errol Morris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Errol_Morris).

In 1993, Spielberg served as an executive producer for the NBC science fiction series *[seaQuest DSV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SeaQuest_DSV);*[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJackson2007104-301] the show was not a hit.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcBride1997380-291] In 1994, he found success producing the medical drama *[ER](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ER_\(TV_series\))*.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJackson2007104-301] That year, Spielberg founded [DreamWorks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DreamWorks_Pictures) with [Jeffrey Katzenberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Katzenberg) and [David Geffen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Geffen).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcBride1997442-168][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJackson200759-162] Spielberg cited greater creative control and distribution improvements as the main reasons for founding his own studio;[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcBride1997445-302] he and his partners compared themselves to the founders of [United Artists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Artists) in 1919.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJackson200760-303] DreamWorks' investors included [Microsoft](19001.md) founders [Paul Allen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_Allen) and [Bill Gates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_Gates).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMara201455-304] After founding DreamWorks, Spielberg continued to operate Amblin Entertainment and direct films for other studios.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJackson200761-305] He helped design [*Jurassic Park*: The Ride](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jurassic_Park:_The_Ride) at [Universal Studios Florida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universal_Studios_Florida).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJackson200765-306] The workload of filmmaking and operating a studio raised questions about his commitments, but Spielberg maintained that "this is all fitting nicely into my life and I'm still home by six and I'm still home on the weekends."[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcBride1997447-307][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcBride1997445-302][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJackson200760-303] In 1998, [DreamWorks Animation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DreamWorks_Animation) produced its first full-length animated features, *[Antz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antz)* and *[The Prince of Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Prince_of_Egypt)*. *[Shrek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shrek)* (2001) was the first winner of the [Academy Award for Best Animated Feature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_Award_for_Best_Animated_Feature).

Spielberg and Tom Hanks produced [*Band of Brothers*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Band_of_Brothers_\(TV_miniseries\)) (2001), a ten-part [HBO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HBO) miniseries based on [Stephen E. Ambrose](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_E._Ambrose)'s [book](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Band_of_Brothers_\(book\)).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJackson200773-308] It follows [Easy Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Easy_Company) of the [101st Airborne Division](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/101st_Airborne_Division)'s 506th Parachute Infantry Regiment. It won a Golden Globe for [Best Miniseries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_Globe_Award_for_Best_Miniseries_or_Television_Film).[^cite_note-309] He produced *[Memoirs of a Geisha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Memoirs_of_a_Geisha_\(film\))* (2005), an adaptation of [Arthur Golden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arthur_Golden)'s [novel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Memoirs_of_a_Geisha).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJackson2007103-310] Spielberg and Zemeckis executive-produced *[Monster House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monster_House_\(film\))* (2006), marking their eighth collaboration. He co-produced [Clint Eastwood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clint_Eastwood)'s *[Flags of Our Fathers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flags_of_Our_Fathers_\(film\))* and *[Letters from Iwo Jima](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Letters_from_Iwo_Jima)* (2006). Spielberg served as executive producer for *[Disturbia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disturbia_\(film\)),* (2007) and the *[Transformers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transformers_\(film_series\))* film series.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJackson2007103-310] That same year, Spielberg and [Mark Burnett](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Burnett) co-produced *[On the Lot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/On_the_Lot),* a reality and competition show about filmmaking.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEdge2008102-205] Spielberg returned to the World War II theme, co-producing the 2010 miniseries *[The Pacific](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Pacific_\(miniseries\))* with Hanks and [Gary Goetzman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gary_Goetzman). It is centered on the battles in the [Pacific Theater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_War).[^cite_note-311] The next year, Spielberg co-created *[Falling Skies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Falling_Skies)*, a science fiction series on [TNT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TNT_\(American_TV_network\)), with [Robert Rodat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Rodat)[^cite_note-312] and produced the 2011 [Fox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fox_Broadcasting_Company) series *[Terra Nova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terra_Nova_\(TV_series\))*[^cite_note-313][^cite_note-314] and [J. J. Abrams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J._J._Abrams)'s *[Super 8](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_8_\(2011_film\))*.[^cite_note-315]

In January 2013, HBO confirmed that it was developing a World War II miniseries based on the book [Donald L. Miller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_L._Miller)'s *Masters of the Air* with Spielberg and Hanks.[^cite_note-316] *[NME](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NME)* reported in March 2017 that production was under the working title *The Mighty Eighth*.[^cite_note-317] By 2019, it was confirmed development of the miniseries, now titled *[Masters of the Air](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Masters_of_the_Air)*, had moved to [Apple TV+](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apple_TV+).[^cite_note-318] The series premiered on January 26, 2024. On January 18, 2023, Spielberg told press at a red carpet event for *The Fabelmans* that he was executive producing a documentary about John Williams, directed by [Laurent Bouzereau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laurent_Bouzereau) with production companies Amblin Television, [Imagine Documentaries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imagine_Documentaries), and Nedland Media.[^cite_note-\:2-319][^cite_note-320][^cite_note-321][^cite_note-322] Other executive producers for the film include [Brian Grazer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brian_Grazer), [Ron Howard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ron_Howard), Darryl Frank, Justin Falvey, [Justin Wilkes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Justin_Wilkes), Sara Bernstein, and Meredith Kaulfers.[^cite_note-\:2-319] The announcement came days after Williams suggested that he might not retire from film scoring as he had previously announced.[^cite_note-323][^cite_note-324] The film, *[Music by John Williams](78022550.md)*, premiered at the 2024 [AFI Fest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AFI_Fest).

### Upcoming and prospective projects

See also: [Steven Spielberg's unrealized projects](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steven_Spielberg's_unrealized_projects)

In May 2009, Spielberg bought the rights to the life story of [Martin Luther King Jr.](20076.md), with the intention of serving as both producer and director.[^cite_note-325] The purchase was made from the King estate, led by son [Dexter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dexter_King), while the two other surviving children, the [Reverend Bernice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernice_King) and [Martin III](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Luther_King_III), immediately threatened to sue, not having given their approvals to the project.[^cite_note-326] In March 2013, Spielberg announced that he was developing a [miniseries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miniseries) based on the life of [Napoleon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Napoleon).[^cite_note-327] In May 2016, it was announced that [Cary Joji Fukunaga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cary_Joji_Fukunaga) was in talks to direct the miniseries for HBO, from a script by [David Leland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Leland) based on extensive research materials accumulated by [Stanley Kubrick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stanley_Kubrick) over the years.[^cite_note-328]

Spielberg was set to film an adaptation of [David I. Kertzer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_I._Kertzer)'s *The Kidnapping of [Edgardo Mortara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edgardo_Mortara)* in early 2017, for release at the end of that year,[^cite_note-329] but production was ultimately postponed. It was first announced in 2014, with [Tony Kushner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tony_Kushner) adapting the book for the screen.[^cite_note-330] [Mark Rylance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Rylance), in his fourth collaboration with Spielberg, was announced to star in the role of [Pope Pius IX](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Pius_IX). Spielberg saw more than 2,000 children to play the role of the young Edgardo Mortara.[^cite_note-331] In 2015, it was announced that Spielberg was attached to direct an adaptation of American photojournalist [Lynsey Addario](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lynsey_Addario)'s memoir *It's What I Do,* with [Jennifer Lawrence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jennifer_Lawrence) in the lead role.[^cite_note-332] In April 2018, it was announced that Spielberg would direct a film adaptation of the *[Blackhawk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blackhawk_\(DC_Comics\))* comic book series. [Warner Bros.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warner_Bros._Pictures) would distribute the film with David Koepp writing the script.[^cite_note-333]

On June 21, 2021, it was announced that [Amblin Entertainment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amblin_Entertainment) signed a deal with [Netflix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netflix) to release multiple new feature films for the [streaming service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Streaming_service). Under the deal, Amblin is expected to produce at least two films a year for Netflix for an unspecified number of years.[^cite_note-334] In February 2022, *[Deadline Hollywood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deadline_Hollywood)* reported that Spielberg was developing an original film centered around the character Frank Bullitt, a fictional San Francisco police officer originally portrayed by [Steve McQueen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steve_McQueen) in the 1968 film *[Bullitt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bullitt)*. The screenplay is set to be written by [Josh Singer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Josh_Singer), who previously co-wrote *The Post* for Spielberg. McQueen's son [Chad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chad_McQueen) and granddaughter Molly will serve as executive producers.[^cite_note-335] [Bradley Cooper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bradley_Cooper) was cast as Bullitt in November 2022 and will also serve as producer alongside Spielberg and [Kristie Macosko Krieger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kristie_Macosko_Krieger).[^cite_note-336]

On February 14, 2025, it was announced that *The Goonies 2* was in the works with Spielberg producing.[^cite_note-337] On November 6, 2025, it was announced that Spielberg will serve as an executive producer for *Gremlins 3* which will be released on November 19, 2027.[^cite_note-338]

During the 2026 [South by Southwest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_by_Southwest), Spielberg confirmed that his next major project will be a [Western film](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_film). He stated that it will subvert the classic mythology of the [American frontier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_frontier) without relying on the genre's typical stereotypes.[^cite_note-339]

### Video games

Spielberg has been an avid gamer since 1974. He played many of [LucasArts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LucasArts) [adventure games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adventure_game), including the first *Monkey Island* games.[^cite_note-340][^cite_note-341]

In 1995, Spielberg helped create and design [LucasArts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LucasArts)' adventure game *[The Dig](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Dig_\(video_game\))*.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcBride1997507-299] He also collaborated with software publishers [Knowledge Adventure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Knowledge_Adventure) on the game *[Steven Spielberg's Director's Chair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steven_Spielberg's_Director's_Chair)*, which was released in 1996; Spielberg appears in the game to direct the player.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcBride1997441-342]

In 2005, Spielberg collaborated with [Electronic Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electronic_Arts) (EA) on several games including one for the [Wii](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wii) called *[Boom Blox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boom_Blox),* and its sequel *[Boom Blox Bash Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boom_Blox_Bash_Party)*.[^cite_note-343][^cite_note-344] He is also the creator of EA's *[Medal of Honor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medal_of_Honor_\(video_game_series\))* series.[^cite_note-345]

He dislikes the use of [cutscenes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cutscene) in games, stating in a 2008 interview that "The second the game is returned to you and it's under your control, you forget everything the interstitials are trying to impact you with, and you just go back to shooting things, and that has not found its way into a universal narrative, and I think more has to be done in that arena. \[...] I applaud them for at least attempting to tell a story."[^cite_note-346]

### Theatre

Spielberg first ventured into theatre producing in 1997, with his involvement on a production of *[The Diary of Anne Frank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Diary_of_Anne_Frank_\(play\))*, as well as the original 1998 production of *[The Farnsworth Invention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Farnsworth_Invention)*. In 2022, he made his Broadway producing debut as a co-producer on the musical *[A Strange Loop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A_Strange_Loop)*.[^cite_note-347] He went on to produce the stage musical adaptations of *[Water for Elephants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_for_Elephants_\(musical\))* and *[Death Becomes Her](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_Becomes_Her_\(musical\))* alongside his wife Kate Capshaw, both in 2024.[^cite_note-348] He will next co-produce the upcoming stage adaptation of *Smash*, based on the 2012 [NBC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBC) [television series of the same name](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smash_\(TV_series\)), on which he served as an executive producer. It was set to begin performances in 2025.[^cite_note-349][^cite_note-350]

## Style, themes and influences

> "I didn't go to film school. I was self-taught. But I had great teachers, you know? All my influencers were the directors and the writers of the movies I was watching in theaters and on television. And my film school was really the cultural heritage of Hollywood and international filmmaking because there's no better teacher than [Lubitsch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lubitsch) or [Hitchcock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hitchcock) or [Kurosawa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurosawa) or [Kubrick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kubrick), you know, or [Ford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Ford) or [William Wyler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Wyler) or [Billy Wilder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Billy_Wilder) or [Clarence Brown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clarence_Brown) – I mean, [Val Lewton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Val_Lewton). I mean, those were my teachers."

— Steven Spielberg, *[Fresh Air](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fresh_Air)* interview

[^cite_note-FreshAir-20]

Spielberg cites John Ford as a formative influence: "I try to rent a John Ford film... before I start every movie, simply because he inspires me.... He's like a classic painter, he celebrates the frame, not just what's inside it."[^cite_note-\:BFI-52] He names [Frank Capra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frank_Capra)'s [*It's A Wonderful Life*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/It's_a_Wonderful_Life) (1946) as an influence on themes of "family, community and suburbia".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBaxter199620-351] He enjoyed the work of Alfred Hitchcock,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcBride199781-47][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHook20106-352] [David Lean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Lean),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFreer2001148-353] Stanley Kubrick and [John Frankenheimer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Frankenheimer).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcBride1997263-354][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJackson2007xvii-355] In college, he was inspired by foreign films by [Ingmar Bergman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ingmar_Bergman), [Jacques Tati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacques_Tati) and [François Truffaut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fran%C3%A7ois_Truffaut), casting the last in *Close Encounters of the Third Kind*.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcBride1997143-356] [Spencer Tracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spencer_Tracy) has also influenced the characters of Spielberg's films. *A Guy Named Joe* was a chidlhood favorite, and a scene can be seen in *Poltergeist*. Spielberg remade it as *Always*.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBaxter199619-357] as did *[The Twilight Zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Twilight_Zone)*.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcBride1997170-68] He says *Lawrence of Arabia* is the film he's seen more times than any other.[^cite_note-358] With [Martin Scorsese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Scorsese), Spielberg helped with the restoration of *Lawrence* by [Robert A. Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_A._Harris).[^cite_note-359] Among films by his contemporaries, Spielberg was influenced by [Francis Ford Coppola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francis_Ford_Coppola)'s *[The Godfather](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Godfather)*: "I was pulverized by the story and the effect the film had on me... I also felt that I should quit, that there was no reason I should continue directing because I would never achieve that level of confidence and ability to tell a story."[^cite_note-360] In 2006, Coppola contacted Spielberg about restoring the film; Spielberg then contacted *Paramount* studio head [Brad Grey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brad_Grey), who approved the project (it was released in 2008).[^cite_note-361][^cite_note-362][^cite_note-363] In 1982, Spielberg bought one of the prop sleds from *[Citizen Kane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Citizen_Kane)*. Spielberg called *Kane* 'the most classic movie ever made" and the sled "a symbolic emblem of quality in the film business".[^cite_note-364]

### Method and themes

Spielberg often uses [storyboards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Storyboard) to visualize sequences, eschewing them for *E.T.* and *The Color Purple* for a more spontaneous effect.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFreer2001112-365][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFreer2001149-366] After filming *Jaws*, Spielberg learned to save special effects scenes until last and to exclude the media from filming locations.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBaxter1996160-367] Spielberg prefers to shoot quickly, with large amounts of footage so that he will have many options in the editing room.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMairata201869-368] From the beginning of his career, Spielberg's shooting style consisted of extreme high and low camera angles, [long takes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Long_take) and [handheld](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hand-held_camera) cameras.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBuckland200669-369] He favors [wide-angle lens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wide-angle_lens) for creating depth,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBuckland2006205-370] and by the time he was making *Minority Report*, he was more confident with elaborate camera movements.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBuckland2006202-371]

&#x20;In an interview with *[The Tech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Tech_\(newspaper\))* in 2015, Spielberg described how he chooses his film projects:

> \[Sometimes], a story speaks to me, even if it doesn't speak to any of my collaborators or any of my partners, who look at me and scratch their heads and say, "Gee, are you sure you wanna get into that trench for a year and a half?" I love people challenging me that way because it's a real test about my own convictions and \[whether] I can be the standing man of my own life and take a stand on a subject that may not be popular, but that I would be proud to add to the body of my work. That's pretty much the litmus test that gets me to say, "Yeah, I'll direct that one."[^cite_note-372]

Spielberg's films contain many recurrent themes. One of the most pertinent revolves around "ordinary people in extraordinary circumstances".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcBride1997263-354][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFreer200176-373] The ordinary people often have limitations, but they succeed in becoming a "hero".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFreer200176-373] A consistent theme in his family-friendly work is a childlike sense of wonder and faith, and "the goodness in humanity will prevail".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFreer200176-373] He has also explored the importance of childhood, loss of innocence and the need for parental figures.[^cite_note-374] In exploring the parent-child relationship, there is usually a flawed or irresponsible father figure. This theme personally resonates with Spielberg's childhood.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcBride1997181-375] Exploring extraterrestrial life is another aspect to his work. Spielberg described himself as like an "alien" during childhood,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcBride199767-376] and this interest came from his father, a science fiction fan.[^cite_note-377] He recalls: "My parents split up when I was 15 or 16 years old, and I needed a special friend, and had to use my imagination to take me to places that felt good – that helped me move beyond the problems my parents were having, and that ended our family as a whole. And thinking about that time, I thought, an extraterrestrial character would be the perfect springboard to purge the pain of your parents' splitting up."[^cite_note-Ebert-37]

### Collaborators



Spielberg has collaborated on numerous projects with Tom Hanks since *Saving Private Ryan*

[Michael Kahn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Kahn_\(film_editor\)) has edited all of Spielberg's films since 1977, with the exception of *E.T.* (1982) and *Disclosure Day* (2026). Spielberg has also worked consistently with production designer [Rick Carter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rick_Carter) and writer [David Koepp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Koepp). The producer [Kathleen Kennedy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kathleen_Kennedy_\(producer\)) is one of Spielberg's longest serving collaborators.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMairata201864-378] Spielberg also displays loyalty to his actors, casting them repeatedly, including [Tom Hanks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tom_Hanks), [Harrison Ford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harrison_Ford), [Mark Rylance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Rylance), [Richard Dreyfuss](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Dreyfuss) and [Tom Cruise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tom_Cruise).[^cite_note-379][^cite_note-380] In 2005, Cruise called him "the greatest storyteller cinema's ever known".[^cite_note-381]

Hanks has collaborated with Spielberg on various projects in both film and television, starting with *Saving Private Ryan*. The pair also executive produced the war miniseries *[Band of Brothers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Band_of_Brothers_\(miniseries\))* (2001) and *[The Pacific](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Pacific_\(miniseries\))* (2010), both of which gained them [Primetime Emmy Awards](6579025.md).[^cite_note-382][^cite_note-383]

[Janusz Kamiński](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Janusz_Kami%C5%84ski) has served as a cinematographer on dozens of Spielberg's films.[^cite_note-384] Kamiński's first collaboration with Spielberg started with the Holocaust drama *Schindler's List* for which Kamiński received the [Academy Award for Best Cinematography](61834.md). The film used [black-and-white](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black-and-white) cinematography. As Spielberg's career evolved from action to drama films, he and Kamiński adopted more handheld camerawork, as evidenced in *Schindler's List* and *Amistad*.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFreer2001229,_256-385] Kamiński would later receive his second Academy Award for cinematography on *Saving Private Ryan*.[^cite_note-The_71st_Academy_Awards_1999-179] The film's opening sequence to re-enact the [invasion of Normandy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Invasion_of_Normandy) was praised for realism. Kamiński garnered three more Academy Award nominations for his work on *War Horse* (2011), the historical epic *Lincoln* (2012), and *West Side Story* (2021).[^cite_note-386][^cite_note-387]

Spielberg's long-time partnership with composer [John Williams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Williams) began with *The Sugarland Express*.[^cite_note-388] Williams would return to compose all but five of Spielberg's feature films (the exceptions are *Twilight Zone: The Movie*, *The Color Purple*, *Bridge of Spies*, *Ready Player One* and *West Side Story*). Williams won three of his five Academy Awards for Best Original Score for his work on Spielberg's films: *Jaws*, *E.T. the Extra-Terrestrial* and *Schindler's List*. While making *Schindler's List*, Spielberg approached Williams about composing the score. After seeing a rough, unedited cut, Williams was impressed, and said that composing would be too challenging. He said to Spielberg, "You need a better composer than I am for this film." Spielberg responded, "I know. But they're all dead!"[^cite_note-389] In 2016, Spielberg presented Williams with the 44th [AFI Life Achievement Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AFI_Life_Achievement_Award), the first to be awarded to a composer.[^cite_note-390]

## Personal life

Spielberg met actress [Amy Irving](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amy_Irving) in 1976 when she auditioned for *Close Encounters of the Third Kind*. After meeting her, Spielberg told his co-producer [Julia Phillips](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julia_Phillips), "I met a real heartbreaker last night."[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcBride1997293-391] Although she was too young for the role, she and Spielberg began dating and she eventually moved into what she described as his "bachelor funky" house.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcBride1997294-392] They broke up in 1979.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJackson200743-115] In 1984, they renewed their romance and married in November 1985. A son had been born on June 13 of that year.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcBride1997362,_363-393] In 1989, the couple divorced; they agreed to live near each other to share custody of the child.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcBride1997403-138] Their divorce settlement is one of the [most expensive in history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_most_expensive_divorces).[^cite_note-394][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJackson200743-115]

Spielberg met actress [Kate Capshaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kate_Capshaw) when he cast her in *Indiana Jones and the Temple of Doom* in 1984. They married on October 12, 1991; Capshaw [converted](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conversion_to_Judaism) to Judaism before their marriage.[^cite_note-395][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJackson200749-396] Spielberg said he rediscovered "the honor of being a Jew" when they married.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPogrebin200525-397] He said, "Kate is Protestant and she insisted on converting to Judaism. She spent a year studying, did the [mikveh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mikveh), the whole thing. She chose to do a full conversion *before* we were married in 1991, and she married me after becoming a Jew. I think that, more than anything else, brought me back to Judaism."[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPogrebin200525-397] He credits her for the family's level of observance;[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPogrebin200526-398] "This [shiksa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goy) goddess has made me a better Jew than my own parents", he said.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPogrebin200527-399] Capshaw was commissioned by the [National Portrait Gallery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Portrait_Gallery_\(United_States\)) to paint a portrait of Spielberg. Her 2025 artwork, *[The Picture Maker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Picture_Maker)*, includes the portrait and a video projection displaying excerpts from films shot by Spielberg during his youth.[^cite_note-thepicturemaker-400]

He has five children with Capshaw: [Sasha Rebecca Spielberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sasha_Spielberg) (born May 14, 1990), [Sawyer Avery Spielberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sawyer_Spielberg) (born March 10, 1992),[^cite_note-401] and [Destry Allyn Spielberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Destry_Allyn_Spielberg) (born December 1, 1996), and two adopted children.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJackson200742-113] He also has a stepdaughter, [Jessica Capshaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jessica_Capshaw) (born August 9, 1976). He and his family live in [Pacific Palisades, California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_Palisades,_California)[^cite_note-402] and [East Hampton, New York](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Hampton_\(village\),_New_York).[^cite_note-403] He is the [godfather](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Godparent) of [Drew Barrymore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drew_Barrymore) and [Gwyneth Paltrow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gwyneth_Paltrow).[^cite_note-404][^cite_note-405][^cite_note-406][^cite_note-407][^cite_note-408][^cite_note-409]

Spielberg was diagnosed with [dyslexia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dyslexia) at age 60.[^cite_note-410] In 2022, at age 75, Spielberg was diagnosed with [COVID-19](63030231.md) but recovered.[^cite_note-411]

In 2013, Spielberg purchased the 282-foot (86 m) mega-yacht *The Seven Seas* for US\$182 million. During his ownership it was also available for [charter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yacht_charter) at US\$1.2 million per month, making it one of the most expensive charters on the market at the time. In 2021, the Canadian steel mogul [Barry Zekelman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barry_Zekelman) bought it for US\$150 million and rechristened the ship *Man of Steel*.[^cite_note-412] Thereafter, Spielberg ordered a new 358-foot (109 m) *Seven Seas*.[^cite_note-413]

In December 2022, Spielberg was a guest on *[Desert Island Discs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Desert_Island_Discs)* for BBC Radio 4, choosing for his luxury item an H-8 Bolex Camera.[^cite_note-414]

### Stalkers

In 1997, Jonathan Norman stalked Spielberg and attempted to enter his home while in possession of a "rape kit";[^cite_note-BBC-415] Norman was subsequently jailed for 25 years. Prosecutors described Norman as "sexually obsessed" with the director. Spielberg told the court he feared Norman intended to "rape or maim him".[^cite_note-BBC-415][^cite_note-416] In 2001, Spielberg was stalked by [conspiracy theorist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conspiracy_theorist) and former social worker Diana Napolis. She accused him and actress [Jennifer Love Hewitt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jennifer_Love_Hewitt) of installing a [mind-control](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brainwashing) device in her brain and being part of a [satanic cult](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Satanic_cult).[^cite_note-417] Napolis was [committed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Involuntary_commitment) to a mental institution, and pled guilty to stalking. She was released on [probation](406786.md) with a condition that she have no contact with either Spielberg or Hewitt.[^cite_note-Sauer2002-418][^cite_note-DeYoung-419]

## Political views and activism

Spielberg has usually supported [Democratic Party](5043544.md) candidates. He has donated over \$800,000 to the Democratic Party and its nominees. He has been a close friend of former President [Bill Clinton](3356.md) and worked with the president for the USA Millennium celebrations. He directed an 18-minute film for the project, scored by [John Williams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Williams) and titled *The American Journey*. It was shown at America's Millennium Gala on December 31, 1999, in the [National Mall](167796.md) at the [Lincoln Memorial Reflecting Pool](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lincoln_Memorial_Reflecting_Pool) in [Washington, D.C.](108956.md)[^cite_note-420] Spielberg endorsed [Hillary Clinton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hillary_Clinton), Clinton's wife, in the [2016 presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_United_States_presidential_election); he donated \$1 million to [Priorities USA Action](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Priorities_USA_Action).[^cite_note-421]



Secretary of Defense [William S. Cohen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_S._Cohen) escorts Spielberg through a military honor cordon into the Pentagon in 1999

&#x20;Spielberg resigned as a member of the national advisory board of the [Boy Scouts of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boy_Scouts_of_America) in 2001 because he disagreed with the organization's [anti-homosexuality stance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boy_Scouts_of_America_membership_controversies#Position_on_homosexuality).[^cite_note-422][^cite_note-423] In 2007, the [Arab League](52625.md) voted to boycott Spielberg's movies after he donated \$1 million for relief efforts in Israel during the [2006 Lebanon War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_Lebanon_War).[^cite_note-424][^cite_note-425] On February 20, 2007, Spielberg, [Jeffrey Katzenberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Katzenberg), and [David Geffen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Geffen) invited Democrats to a fundraiser for [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama).[^cite_note-426]

In February 2008, Spielberg resigned as advisor to the [2008 Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Summer_Olympics) in response to the Chinese government's inaction over the [War in Darfur](5043637.md).[^cite_note-427] Spielberg said in a statement, "I find that my conscience will not allow me to continue business as usual \[...] Sudan's government bears the bulk of the responsibility for these on-going crimes, but the international community, and particularly China, should be doing more."[^cite_note-428] The [International Olympic Committee](15147.md) (IOC) respected Spielberg's decision but IOC president [Jacques Rogge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacques_Rogge) expressed disappointment: "\[Spielberg] certainly would have brought a lot to the opening ceremony in terms of creativity."[^cite_note-429] Chinese state media called Spielberg's comments "unfair".[^cite_note-430]

In September 2008, Spielberg and his wife offered their support to [same-sex marriage in California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_California) by issuing a statement following their donation of \$100,000 to the "No on [Proposition 8](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proposition_8)" campaign fund, a figure equal to the amount of money [Brad Pitt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brad_Pitt) donated to the same campaign less than a week prior.[^cite_note-431] In 2018, Spielberg and his wife donated \$500,000 to the [March for Our Lives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/March_for_Our_Lives) student demonstration in favor of gun control in the United States.[^cite_note-432]

In December 2023, after the [October 7 attacks](74998317.md), the [Shoah Foundation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shoah_Foundation), which was founded by Spielberg, said that it had gathered over 100 video testimonies of those who experienced the attacks on that day to add them to the collection of "[Holocaust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holocaust) survivor and witness testimony."[^cite_note-433] Speaking of the attacks he said, "I never imagined I would see such unspeakable barbarity against [Jews](25955086.md) in my lifetime" and that the Shoah Foundation project will ensure "that their stories would be recorded and shared in the effort to preserve history and to work toward a world without [Antisemitism](1078.md) or hate of any kind".[^cite_note-434]

## Awards and honors

See also: [List of awards and nominations received by Steven Spielberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_awards_and_nominations_received_by_Steven_Spielberg)



Spielberg receiving a public service award presented by US Secretary of Defense [William Cohen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Cohen), 1999

*Jaws*, *Close Encounters of the Third Kind*, *E.T*, *Raiders of the Lost Ark* and *Schindler's List* are included on the [AFI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Film_Institute)'s [100 Years...100 Movies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/100_Years...100_Movies).[^cite_note-435]

Spielberg has won three Academy Awards. He received nine nominations for Best Director, and won twice (for *Schindler's List* and *Saving Private Ryan*).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJackson2007109-436][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJackson2007112-437] His third was in Best Picture, for *Schindler's List.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJackson200758-156]* He is the only director to receive a Best Director nomination from the academy in 6 different decades. In 1987, he was awarded the [Irving G. Thalberg Memorial Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irving_G._Thalberg_Memorial_Award) for his work as a creative producer.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEParish20042-438] Drawing from his own experiences in [Scouting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scouting), Spielberg helped the [Boy Scouts of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boy_Scouts_of_America) develop a merit badge in cinematography to promote filmmaking as a marketable skill; the badge was launched at the 1989 [National Scout Jamboree](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Scout_jamboree_\(Boy_Scouts_of_America\)).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBaxter1996354-439] In 1989, Spielberg was presented with the [Distinguished Eagle Scout Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Distinguished_Eagle_Scout_Award).[^cite_note-440] Spielberg received the [AFI Life Achievement Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AFI_Life_Achievement_Award) in 1995.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJackson2007110-441]

In 1998, he was awarded the [Order of Merit of the Federal Republic of Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Order_of_Merit_of_the_Federal_Republic_of_Germany). The award was presented to him by President [Roman Herzog](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Herzog) in recognition of *Schindler's List* and work with the [Shoah Foundation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shoah_Foundation).[^cite_note-442] Spielberg was awarded the [Medal for Distinguished Public Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medal_for_Distinguished_Public_Service) in 1999, in recognition for *Saving Private Ryan*.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJackson2007111-443] For the same film, he also received an award for Outstanding Directorial Achievement in Motion Pictures by the [Directors Guild of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Directors_Guild_of_America).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJackson2007111-443] The next year, he received the [Lifetime Achievement Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Directors_Guild_of_America_Award) from the Directors Guild of America.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHook201095-444]



Spielberg's shoeprints and handprints in front of the [Grauman's Chinese Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grauman's_Chinese_Theatre)

Spielberg was given a star on the [Hollywood Walk of Fame](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame) in 2003, located on 6801 Hollywood Boulevard.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJackson2007113-445] Additionally, he was awarded the Blessed are the Peacemakers Award from the [Catholic Theological Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_Theological_Union) in 2003.[^cite_note-446] On July 15, 2006, Spielberg was awarded the [Gold Hugo Lifetime Achievement Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_International_Film_Festival#Grand_Prize:_Gold_Hugo) at the Summer Gala of the [Chicago International Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_International_Film_Festival),[^cite_note-447] and was awarded a [Kennedy Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kennedy_Center_Honors) honor on December 3.[^cite_note-448] The tribute to Spielberg featured a biographical short film narrated by [Liam Neeson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liam_Neeson), and a performance of the finale to [Leonard Bernstein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leonard_Bernstein)'s *[Candide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Candide_\(operetta\))*, conducted by John Williams.[^cite_note-449]

The [Science Fiction Hall of Fame](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Science_Fiction_and_Fantasy_Hall_of_Fame) inducted Spielberg in 2005, the first year it considered non-literary contributors.[^cite_note-450][^cite_note-451] He was a recipient of the [Visual Effects Society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Visual_Effects_Society) Lifetime Achievement Award in February 2008; it is awarded for "significant and lasting contributions to the art and science of the visual effects industry".[^cite_note-452] In 2009, Spielberg was awarded the [Cecil B. DeMille Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cecil_B._DeMille_Award) by the [Hollywood Foreign Press Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Foreign_Press_Association) for "outstanding contributions to the world of entertainment".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHook201095-444]



Waxwork of Spielberg at [Madame Tussauds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madame_Tussauds), London

In 2001, he was awarded an [honorary knighthood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Honorary_knighthood) ([KBE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Order_of_the_British_Empire)), by [Queen Elizabeth II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Queen_Elizabeth_II) for services to the British film industry.[^cite_note-453][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJackson200797-454] Because he is not a British citizen, he did not kneel to be knighted, nor can he use the honorific prefix "Sir", but he can use the suffix "KBE."[^cite_note-455] *[Premiere](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Premiere_\(magazine\))* ranked him first place in the list of 100 Most Powerful People in Movies in 2003.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEParish20041-456] In 2004, he was awarded France's highest civil honor, the [Legion of Honour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legion_of_Honour) by President [Jacques Chirac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacques_Chirac).[^cite_note-457] In June 2008, Spielberg received [Arizona State University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arizona_State_University)'s Hugh Downs Award for Communication Excellence.[^cite_note-458] In October 2009, Spielberg received the [Philadelphia Liberty Medal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_Liberty_Medal); the prize was presented by former US President [Bill Clinton](3356.md).[^cite_note-459][^cite_note-460] In October 2011, he was made a Commander of the [Order of the Belgian Crown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Order_of_the_Crown_\(Belgium\)), one of Belgium's highest honors.[^cite_note-461] On November 19, 2013, Spielberg was honored by the [National Archives and Records Administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Archives_and_Records_Administration) with a Records of Achievement Award. Spielberg was given two [facsimiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Facsimile) of the [13th Amendment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/13th_Amendment); the first which passed in 1861 but was not ratified, and the second signed by [Abraham Lincoln](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abraham_Lincoln) in 1865 to abolish slavery. The amendment and the process of passing it were the subject of his film *Lincoln*.[^cite_note-462] On November 24, 2015, Spielberg was awarded the [Presidential Medal of Freedom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidential_Medal_of_Freedom) from [President](24113.md) [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama) at the [White House](33057.md).[^cite_note-463]

In July 2016, Spielberg was awarded a gold [Blue Peter badge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blue_Peter_badge) by the BBC children's television program *[Blue Peter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blue_Peter).*[^cite_note-464] He has honorary degrees from the [University of Southern California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Southern_California), 1994;[^cite_note-465] [Brown University](4157.md), 1999;[^cite_note-466] [Yale University](34273.md), 2002;[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJackson2007113-445] [Boston University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boston_University), 2009;[^cite_note-467] and [Harvard University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harvard_University), 2016.[^cite_note-468]

In [June 2022](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/75th_Tony_Awards), Spielberg won his first Tony Award nomination for co-producing the Broadway production of *[A Strange Loop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A_Strange_Loop)*, winning [Best Musical](249875.md).[^cite_note-Davis-4] He received a further two nominations in the same category for producing *[Water for Elephants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_for_Elephants_\(musical\))* (2024), and *[Death Becomes Her](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_Becomes_Her_\(musical\))* (2025).

On November 15, 2025, Spielberg was honored with the [National Portrait Gallery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Portrait_Gallery_\(United_States\))'s Portrait of a Nation Award. The Award recognizes the honoree "for their transformative contributions to American history and culture"[^cite_note-469] and was presented to him by artist [Amy Sherald](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amy_Sherald). The National Portrait Gallery commissioned artist [Kate Capshaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kate_Capshaw) to paint Spielberg's portrait for inclusion in the Gallery's collection.

In February 2026, Spielberg achieved [EGOT](361388.md) status for receiving the [Grammy Award for Best Music Film](586170.md) for producing *[Music by John Williams](78022550.md)*.[^cite_note-Davis-4]

## Legacy



Spielberg's star on the Hollywood Walk of Fame

An important figure of the [New Hollywood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Hollywood) era,[^cite_note-470] Spielberg is widely regarded as one of the most influential and commercially successful film directors of all time. Some of his films were in the top ten highest-grossing films of the 1970s and 1980s, with *Jaws*, *E.T. the Extra-Terrestrial* and *Jurassic Park* all becoming the [highest-grossing film](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_highest-grossing_films) at the time of their respective releases.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEParish20041-456][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHook20107-471][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFreer2001vi-472] In 1996, *[Life](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Life_\(magazine\))* magazine named Spielberg the most influential person of his generation.[^cite_note-473] In 2003, *[Premiere](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Premiere_\(magazine\))* magazine ranked him first place in the list of 100 Most Powerful People in Movie&#x73;*.*[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEParish20041-456] In 2005, *[Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Empire_\(magazine\))* magazine ranked him number one on a list of the greatest film directors of all time.[^cite_note-474] In 2013, *[Time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_\(magazine\))* magazine listed him as one of the [100 most influential](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_100) people.[^cite_note-475] According to *[Forbes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbes)*' magazine of Most Influential Celebrities of 2014, Spielberg was ranked first.[^cite_note-Independent-_Most_influential-476][^cite_note-Influential_celebrities-477][^cite_note-Pomerantz-478] As of December 2024, *Forbes* estimates his net worth at \$5.3 billion,[^cite_note-Spielberg-3] making him one of the [richest people in the entertainment industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_celebrities_by_net_worth).

His work is admired by numerous acclaimed directors, including [Robert Aldrich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Aldrich),[^cite_note-479] [Ingmar Bergman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ingmar_Bergman),[^cite_note-480] [Werner Herzog](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Werner_Herzog),[^cite_note-481] [Stanley Kubrick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stanley_Kubrick),[^cite_note-482] [David Lean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Lean),[^cite_note-483] [Sidney Lumet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sidney_Lumet),[^cite_note-484] [Roman Polanski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Polanski),[^cite_note-485] [Martin Scorsese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Scorsese),[^cite_note-486] [François Truffaut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fran%C3%A7ois_Truffaut)[^cite_note-487] and [Jean Renoir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean_Renoir).[^cite_note-488] Spielberg's films have also influenced directors [J. J. Abrams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J._J._Abrams),[^cite_note-489] [Paul Thomas Anderson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_Thomas_Anderson),[^cite_note-490] [Sean Baker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sean_Baker),[^cite_note-491] [Brad Bird](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brad_Bird),[^cite_note-ind15-492] [Neill Blomkamp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neill_Blomkamp),[^cite_note-493] [Jon M. Chu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jon_M._Chu),[^cite_note-494][^cite_note-495] [Arnaud Desplechin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arnaud_Desplechin),[^cite_note-496] [Gareth Edwards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gareth_Edwards_\(filmmaker\)),[^cite_note-497] [Roland Emmerich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roland_Emmerich),[^cite_note-498] [Enrique Gato](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enrique_Gato),[^cite_note-499] [Don Hertzfeldt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Don_Hertzfeldt),[^cite_note-500] [Peter Jackson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_Jackson),[^cite_note-501] Kal Ng,[^cite_note-502] [Jordan Peele](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jordan_Peele),[^cite_note-503] [S. S. Rajamouli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S._S._Rajamouli),[^cite_note-504] [Matt Reeves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matt_Reeves),[^cite_note-505] [Robert Rodriguez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Rodriguez),[^cite_note-506] [John Sayles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Sayles),[^cite_note-507] [Ridley Scott](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ridley_Scott),[^cite_note-508] [John Singleton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Singleton),[^cite_note-509] [Kevin Smith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kevin_Smith),[^cite_note-510] and [Michael Williams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Williams_\(film_director\)).[^cite_note-511] In 2004, film critic [Tom Shone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tom_Shone) said of Spielberg, "If you have to point to any one director of the last twenty-five years \[1979–2004] in whose work the medium of film was most fully itself–where we found out what it does best when left to its own devices, it has to be that guy."[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShone200480-512] Jess Cagle, former editor of *[Entertainment Weekly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Entertainment_Weekly)*, called Spielberg "arguably (well, who would argue?) the greatest filmmaker in history."[^cite_note-513] Stephen Rowley, writing for *[Senses of Cinema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Senses_of_Cinema),* discussed Spielberg's strengths as a filmmaker, saying "there is a welcome complexity of tone and approach in these later films that defies the lazy stereotypes often bandied about his films", and that "Spielberg continues to take risks, with his body of work continuing to grow more impressive and ambitious", concluding that he has only received "limited, begrudging recognition" from critics.[^cite_note-Rowley-514] In a 1999 "Millennium Movies" survey of British film fans run by the [Sky Premier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sky_Premier) channel, Spielberg had seven films in the top 100, which made him the most popular director.[^cite_note-515]

Critics have argued that Spielberg's films are commonly sentimental and [moralistic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moralism).[^cite_note-516][^cite_note-517][^cite_note-Rowley-514] In the book *[Easy Riders, Raging Bulls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Easy_Riders,_Raging_Bulls)*, [Peter Biskind](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_Biskind) wrote that Spielberg is "infantilizing the audience, reconstituting the spectator as child, then overwhelming him and her with sound and spectacle, obliterating irony, aesthetic self-consciousness, and critical reflection".[^cite_note-518] Critic [Ray Carney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ray_Carney) and actor [Crispin Glover](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crispin_Glover) opined that Spielberg's works are not risky and lack depth.[^cite_note-519][^cite_note-520] Filmmaker [Jean-Luc Godard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean-Luc_Godard) opined that Spielberg was partly responsible for the lack of artistic merit in mainstream cinema, and accused Spielberg of using *Schindler's List* to profit from a tragedy,[^cite_note-521] despite that Spielberg chose not to take a salary for the film.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcBride1997416-522] In defense of Spielberg, critic [Roger Ebert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roger_Ebert) said "Has Godard or any other director living or dead done more than Spielberg, with his Holocaust Project, to honor and preserve the memories of the survivors?"[^cite_note-523]

Seven of his films have been inducted into the [National Film Registry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Film_Registry) by the [Library of Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Library_of_Congress) as being "culturally, historically, or aesthetically significant": *Jaws, Close Encounters of the Third Kind, Raiders of the Lost Ark, E.T., Jurassic Park, Schindler's List,* and *Saving Private Ryan*.[^cite_note-\:1-10]

## Filmography

Main article: [Steven Spielberg filmography](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steven_Spielberg_filmography)

Prolific in film since the 1960s, Spielberg has directed 36 feature films, and co-produced many works.
